With\n12556|    this function, a JSON Patch is applied to the current JSON value by\n12557|    executing all operations from the patch.\n12558|\n12559|    @param[in] json_patch  JSON patch document\n12560|    @return patched document\n12561|\n12562|    @note The application of a patch is atomic: Either all operations succeed\n12563|          and the patched document is returned or an exception is thrown. In\n12564|          any case, the original value is not changed: the patch is applied\n12565|          to a copy of the value.\n12566|\n12567|    @throw std::out_of_range if a JSON pointer inside the patch could not\n12568|    be resolved successfully in the current JSON value; example: `\"key baz\n12569|    not found\"`\n12570|    @throw invalid_argument if the JSON patch is malformed (e.g., mandatory\n12571|    attributes are missing); example: `\"operation add must have member path\"`\n12572|\n12573|    @complexity Linear in the size of the JSON value and the length of the\n12574|    JSON patch. As usually only a fraction of the JSON value is affected by\n12575|    the patch, the complexity can usually be neglected.\n12576|\n12577|    @liveexample{The following code shows how a JSON patch is applied to a\n12578|    value.,patch}\n12579|\n12580|    @sa @ref diff -- create a JSON patch by comparing two JSON values\n12581|\n12582|    @sa [RFC 6902 (JSON {\n12597|            if (op == \"add\")\n12598|            {\n12599|                return patch_operations::add;\n12600|            }\n12601|            if (op == \"remove\")\n12602|            {\n12603|                return patch_operations::remove;\n12604|            }\n12605|            if (op == \"replace\")\n12606|            {\n12607|                return ptr\n12626|        const auto operation_add = [&result](json_pointer & ptr, basic_json val)\n12627|        {\n12628|            // adding to the root of the target document means replacing it\n12629|            if (ptr.is_root())\n12630|            {\n12631|                result = val;\n12632|            }\n12633|            else\n12634|            {\n12635|                // make sure the top element of the pointer exists\n12636|                json_pointer top_pointer = ptr.top();\n12637|                if (top_pointer != ptr)\n12638|                {\n12639|                    result.at(top_pointer);\n12640|                }\n12641|\n12642|                // get reference to parent of JSON pointer ptr\n12643|                const auto last_path = ptr.pop_back();\n12644|                basic_json& parent = result[ptr];\n12645|\n12646|                switch (parent.m_type)\n12647|                {\n12648|                    case value_t::null:\n12649|                    case value_t::object:\n12650|                    {\n12651|                        // use operator[] to add value\n12652|                        parent[last_path] = val;\n12653|                        break;\n12654|                    }\n12655|\n12656|                    case value_t::array:\n12657|                    {\n12658|                        if (last_path == \"-\")\n12659|                        {\n12660|                            // special case: append to back\n12661|                            parent.push_back(val);\n12662|                        }\n12663| parent.size())\n12667|                            {\n12668|                                // avoid undefined behavior\n12669|                                JSON_THROW(std::out_of_range(\"array index \" + std::to_string(idx) + \" is out of range\"));\n12670|                            }\n12671|                            else\n12672|                            {\n12673|                                // default case: insert add offset\n12674|                                parent.insert(parent.begin() + static_cast<difference_type>(idx), val);\n12675|                            }\n12676|                        }\n12677|                        break;\n12678|                    }\n12679|\n12680|                    default:\n12681|                    {\n12682|                        // if there };\n12688|\n12689|        // wrapper for \"remove\" operation; remove value at ptr\n12690|        const auto operation_remove = [&result](json_pointer & ptr)\n12691|        {\n12692|            // get reference to parent of JSON pointer ptr\n12693|            const auto last_path = ptr.pop_back();\n12694|            basic_json& parent = result.at(ptr);\n12695|\n12696|            // remove child\n12697|            if (parent.is_object())\n12698|            {\n12699|                // perform range check\n12700|                auto it = parent.find(last_path);\n12701|                if         // note erase performs range check\n12713|                parent.erase(static_cast<size_type>(std::stoi(last_path)));\n12714|            }\n12715|        };\n12716|\n12717|        // type \"value\", false);\n12783|                    break;\n12784|                }\n12785|\n12786|                case patch_operations::move:\n12787|                {\n12788|                    const break;\n12811|                }\n12812|\n12813|                case patch_operations::test:\n12814|                {\n12815|                    bool success = false;\n12816|                    {\n12824|                        // ignore out of range errors: success remains false\n12825|                    }\n12826|\n12827|                    // throw an exception if test fails\n12828|                    if (not success)\n12829|                    {\n12830|                        JSON_THROW(std::domain_error(\"unsuccessful: \" + val.dump()));\n12831| patch\n12850|\n12851|    Creates a [JSON Patch](http://jsonpatch.com) so that value @a source can\n12852|    be changed into the value @a target by calling @ref patch function.\n12853|\n12854|    @invariant For two JSON values @a source and @a target, the following code\n12855|    yields always `true`:\n12856|    @code {.cpp}\n12857|    source.patch(diff(source, target)) == target;\n12858|    @endcode\n12859|\n12860|    @note Currently, only `remove`, `add`, and `replace` operations are\n12861|          generated.\n12862|\n12863|    @param[in] source  JSON value to compare from\n12864|    @param[in] target  JSON value to compare against\n12865|    @param[in] path    helper value to create JSON pointers\n12866|\n12867|    @return a JSON patch to convert the @a source to @a target\n12868|\n12869|    @complexity Linear in the lengths of @a source and @a target.\n12870|\n12871|    @liveexample{The following code shows how a JSON patch is created as a\n12872|    diff for two JSON values.,diff}\n12873|\n12874|    @sa @ref patch -- apply a JSON patch\n12875|\n12876|    @sa [RFC 6902 (JSON Patch)](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc6902)\n12877|\n12878|    @since version 2.0.0\n12879|    */\n12880|    static basic_json diff(const basic_json& source,\n12881|                           const basic_json& target,\n12882|                           const std::string& path = \"\")\n12883|    {\n12884|        // the patch\n12885|        basic_json result(value_t::array);\n12886|\n12887|        // if the values are the same, return empty patch\n12888|        if (source == target)\n12889|        {\n12890|            return result;\n12891|        }\n12892|\n12893|        if (source.type() != target.type())\n12894|        {\n12895|            // different types: replace value\n12896|            result.push_back(\n12897|            {\n12898|                {\"op\", \"replace\"},\n12899|                {\"path\", path},\n12900|                {\"value\", target}\n12901|            });\n12902|        }\n12903|        else\n12904|        {\n12905|            switch (source.type())\n12906|            {\n12907|                case value_t::array:\n12908|                {\n12909|                    // first pass: traverse common elements\n12910|                    size_t i = 0;\n12911|                    while (i < source.size() and i < target.size())\n12912|                    {\n12913|                        // recursive call to compare array values at index i\n12914|                        auto temp_diff = diff(source[i], target[i], path + \"/\" + std::to_string(i));\n12915|                        result.insert(result.end(), temp_diff.begin(), temp_diff.end());\n12916|                        ++i;\n12917|                    }\n12918|\n12919|                    // i now reached the end of at least one array\n12920|                    // in a second pass, traverse the remaining elements\n12921|\n12922|                    // remove my remaining elements\n12923|                    const auto end_index = static_cast<difference_type>(result.size());\n12924|                    while (i < source.size())\n12925|                    {\n12926|                        // add operations in reverse order to avoid invalid\n12927|                        // indices\n12928|                        result.insert(result.begin() + end_index, object(\n12929|                        {\n12930|                            {\"op\", \"remove\"},\n12931|                            {\"path\", path + \"/\" + std::to_string(i)}\n12932|                        }));\n12933|                        ++i;\n12934|                    }\n12935|\n12936|                    // add other remaining elements\n12937|                    while (i < target.size())\n12938|                    {\n12939|                        result.push_back(\n12940|                        {\n12941|                            {\"op\", \"add\"},\n12942|                            {\"path\", path + \"/\" + std::to_string(i)},\n12943|                            {\"value\", target[i]}\n12944|                        });\n12945|                        ++i;\n12946|                    }\n12947|\n12948|                    break;\n12949|                }\n12950|\n12951|                case value_t::object:\n12952|                {\n12953|                    // first pass: traverse this object's elements\n12954|                    for (auto it = source.begin(); it != source.end(); ++it)\n12955|                    {\n12956|                        // escape the key name to be used in a JSON patch\n12957|                        const auto key = json_pointer::escape(it.key());\n12958|\n12959|                        if (target.find(it.key()) != target.end())\n12960|                        {\n12961|                            // recursive call to compare object values at key it\n12962|                            auto temp_diff = diff(it.value(), target[it.key()], path + \"/\" + key);\n12963|                            result.insert(result.end(), temp_diff.begin(), temp_diff.end());\n12964|                        }\n12965|                        else\n12966|                        {\n12967|                            // found a key that is not in o -> remove it\n12968|                            result.push_back(object(\n12969|                            {\n12970|                                {\"op\", \"remove\"},\n12971|                                {\"path\", path + \"/\" + key}\n12972|                            }));\n12973|                        }\n12974|                    }\n12975|\n12976|                    // second pass: traverse other object's elements\n12977|                    for (auto it = target.begin(); it != target.end(); ++it)\n12978|                    {\n12979|                        if (source.find(it.key()) == source.end())\n12980|                        {\n12981|                            // found a key that is not in this -> add it\n12982|                            const auto key = json_pointer::escape(it.key());\n12983|                            result.push_back(\n12984|                            {\n12985|                                {\"op\", \"add\"},\n12986|                                {\"path\", path + \"/\" + key},\n12987|                                {\"value\", it.value()}\n12988|                            });\n12989|                        }\n12990|                    }\n12991|\n12992|                    break;\n12993|                }\n12994|\n12995|                default:\n12996|                {\n12997|                    // both primitive type: replace value\n12998|                    result.push_back(\n12999|                    {\n13000|                        {\"op\", \"replace\"},\n13001|                        {\"path\", path},\n13002|                        {\"value\", target}\n13003|                    });\n13004|                    break;\n13005|                }\n13006|            }\n13007|        }\n13008|\n13009|        return result;\n13010|    }\n13011|\n13012|    /// @}\n13013|};\n13014|\n13015|/////////////\n13016|// presets //\n13017|/////////////\n13018|\n13019|/*!\n13020|@brief default JSON class\n13021|\n13022|This type is the default specialization of the h(j.dump());\n13067|    }\n13068|};\n13069|\n13070|/// specialization for std::less<value_t>\n13071|template <>\n13072|struct less<::nlohmann::detail::value_t>\n13073|{\n13074|    /*!\n13075| It\n13091|can be used by adding `\"_json\"` to a string literal and returns a JSON object\n13092|if no parse error occurred.\n13093|\n13094|@param[in] s  a string representation of a JSON object\n13095|@param[in] n  the length of string @a s\n13096|@return a JSON object\n13097|\n13098|@since version 1.0.0\n13099|*/\n13100|inline nlohmann::json operator \"\" _json(const char* s, std::size_t n)\n13101|{\n13102|    return nlohmann::json::parse(s, s + n);\n13103|}\n13104|\n13105|/*!\n13106|@brief user-defined string literal for JSON pointer\n13107|\n13108|This operator implements a user-defined string literal for JSON Pointers.
